# SamoFlow — PROJECT_SPEC (living context — read at the start of every session, update after every task)

## 1. What we are building and why

A production-ready, multi-tenant, embeddable AI widget platform: one website is one tenant, with its own site key(s) and its own `client_id` in Qdrant. The widget answers visitors only from that tenant's own content (crawled pages, listed URLs, uploaded files, synced database data), streams answers in real time, remembers a returning visitor's earlier chats in the same browser, and hands off to a human through an escalation request when the AI cannot help. Full detail: docs/SPEC.md.

## 2. How to work (rules for every session)

1. At the start of a session, read this file and tell me the current task, the next task and any open markers. Do not write code until I say so.
2. One task at a time. Implement only what I name. Do not modify completed tasks.
3. When starting a component, create a skeleton first: stubs that raise NotImplementedError with a TODO(<task-id>) comment explaining what each does, its inputs and its outputs.
4. Keep code simple with short comments. After writing code, add inline markers: ASSUMPTION (something I should verify), UNCERTAIN (something you are unsure about), TODO(<task-id>) (something incomplete). If similar logic already exists, add a NOTE pointing to it instead of duplicating it.
5. Write tests with the code, covering normal cases, edge cases, expected failures and bad inputs (empty, None, wrong type, oversized). Run them and show the real output. Never weaken, skip or delete a test to get green.
6. Finish each task with a report: what changed, test results, deviations from the spec, open questions. Then update this file and stop. Do not start the next task.
7. If the spec is ambiguous, or you want to deviate from it, stop and ask. Record approved deviations under Decisions made.
8. Ask before adding any dependency (name, purpose, alternatives considered).
9. Run a duplication check after every 3rd task, and never go past 4 tasks without one.
10. Clean code means resolved markers: verify then remove ASSUMPTION, resolve then remove UNCERTAIN, finish then remove TODO. A phase is not accepted while any ASSUMPTION or UNCERTAIN marker remains, and every remaining TODO must be listed under Open markers with the task that owns it.
11. Follow widely adopted industry practice and library defaults, including standard security measures: validate input at trust boundaries, use least privilege and secure defaults, keep secrets and sensitive data out of code, logs and error messages, keep dependencies current, and use well-known framework or library mechanisms instead of custom security schemes. Do not add abstractions, options, checks or tests the task does not need. When the choice is between a standard default and a custom rule, use the default unless the spec says otherwise. Security requirements in the spec and in the engineering rules are never optional and always win over simplicity. If you are unsure whether something is needed for security, or you think something is over-engineered, too rigid or insecure, say so and ask me before building it.

## 3. Engineering rules and commands

- Commands: make up, make test, make lint, make migrate, make evals, make hooks (lists TODO, ASSUMPTION, UNCERTAIN and NOTE markers in the code).
- Tenant identity comes only from the verified token. Never accept client_id, visitor id, thread_id or checkpoint ids from the client. Verify conversation_id by database lookup.
- All Qdrant reads go through retrieve(), and the client_id filter is applied inside every sub-query.
- All tenant-scoped database access goes through the tenant-scoped repository helpers.
- The model never writes contact details and has no tool with side effects. Escalation is submitted by the visitor's browser.
- Everything is async. No blocking calls in handlers. Never hold a DB connection while streaming.
- Migrations via Alembic only. Secrets never in the repository.
- Treat all ingested text, tool output and visitor messages as untrusted input.
- Nothing hard-coded that should be configuration (limits, thresholds, paths, URLs).
- Any deviation from the spec is recorded in the decisions log (see SPEC section 3) in the same change.
- After each phase, run the acceptance checks and summarize deviations before starting the next.
- Convention (written down at the duplication check after 2.4.b/c/d, previously applied ad hoc): when a task adds a new test file, run it standalone and re-run it 3 times before trusting the result, to catch any ordering-dependent or flaky behavior a single pass wouldn't surface.
- Convention (written down at the duplication check after 2.5.d/e/f): every decision-log entry, including a duplication check's own consolidated entry, ends with its own `Verified:` paragraph stating the real commands run and their real output — never only a prose summary of what changed. A duplication check's final verification pass happens after all accepted fixes are applied, as a separate step from each fix's own small per-fix check; write that final pass into its own `Verified:` paragraph before closing the entry, not folded into the summary bullet alone.

## 5. Decisions made

- 2026-09-19: Shadow DOM isolates the widget from the host page (SPEC §11).
- 2026-09-19: One website = one tenant, with its own site key(s) and `client_id` (SPEC §4.1).
- 2026-09-19: Visitors are recognized by browser now; logged-in users (`external_user_id`) are a later phase (SPEC §4.1, §19).
- 2026-09-19: A visitor can have several conversations, shown as a "previous chats" list (SPEC §4.5).
- 2026-09-19: Chat retention is per-tenant, default 30 days, allowed range 1–90 days (SPEC §4.6).
- 2026-09-19: Content sources: URL list, crawl, uploads, database sync, any combination (SPEC §5.1).
- 2026-09-19: All sources are answerable to any visitor; a warning is shown when uploads/DB content is added; one-click disable/delete per source (SPEC §5).
- 2026-09-19: Domain ownership is verified once (file, meta tag or DNS), never re-checked; the platform admin can revoke or force re-verification. Revocation stops fetching only — already-indexed content stays answerable until the source is disabled/deleted or the site is suspended (SPEC §5.5).
- 2026-09-19: English only for v1 (SPEC §3).
- 2026-09-19: Refresh cadence is daily/weekly/monthly/quarterly plus refresh-now, gated by plan (SPEC §5.4).
- 2026-09-19: Plan limits live in a `limits` JSON, assigned manually; payments come later (SPEC §5.4).
- 2026-09-19: Crawler is simple (no JS rendering) for v1 (SPEC §5.5, §19).
- 2026-09-19: Upload types: PDF, .docx, .txt/.md (SPEC §5.6).
- 2026-09-19: Database sync is scheduled first; approved live queries are a later phase (SPEC §5.6, §19).
- 2026-09-19: Supported database engines: MySQL/MariaDB and PostgreSQL first (SPEC §5.6).
- 2026-09-19: Hybrid search (dense + sparse BM25, server-side RRF); reranker hook off by default (SPEC §6.3).
- 2026-09-19: Follow-up questions are rewritten by a small fast model; original message used on rewrite failure/timeout (SPEC §6.3).
- 2026-09-19: Three specialists (Retrieval, Fallback & Guardrail, Escalation Coordinator), rule-based routing, no LLM hop (SPEC §6.1–6.2).
- 2026-09-19: The AI has no tools with side effects; escalation is submitted by the visitor's browser (SPEC §8).
- 2026-09-19: We build and run tools first; tenant tool servers are a later phase (SPEC §8, §19).
- 2026-09-19: Unanswerable questions get a fixed fallback with the tenant's support details, never a guess (SPEC §6.3).
- 2026-09-19: Citations are shown for web pages only; no labels for uploads or database content (SPEC §6.3).
- 2026-09-19: Human handoff is an escalation request (email + dashboard inbox), retained 12 months by default, allowed range 1–24 months (SPEC §7).
- 2026-09-19: Monthly quota counts AI-answered messages only; tenant alerted at 80% and 100% (SPEC §9).
- 2026-09-19: Abuse defense is rate limits only for v1; a challenge (e.g. Turnstile) is deferred (SPEC §9, §19).
- 2026-09-19: A dropped connection cancels the run with no partial answer saved; retry reuses the same `client_message_id` (SPEC §10).
- 2026-09-19: Dashboard login is email/password, with an owner who can invite team members (SPEC §12).
- 2026-09-19: Widget customization is limited to colors, logo, title, greeting and position; no custom CSS in v1 (SPEC §11).
- 2026-09-19: Hosting is one Ubuntu server with Docker Compose for v1 (SPEC §14, §19).
- 2026-09-19: Phoenix trace text is stored 30 days then auto-deleted, with a per-tenant opt-out (SPEC §13).
- 2026-09-19: Plan page cap counts total indexed web pages only (crawl + URL-list); uploads are capped by storage/size and database sync by row count (SPEC §5.4, §5.5).
- 2026-09-19: Supported database engines confirmed as MySQL/MariaDB and PostgreSQL (SPEC §5.6).
- 2026-09-19: The relevance threshold is a platform default; only the platform admin can override it per tenant, and it is not tenant-editable (SPEC §6.3).
- 2026-09-19: Email is sent through our own SMTP server, behind a provider-neutral interface (SPEC §14).
- 2026-09-19: Escalation trigger phrases live in a YAML file in the repo (roughly 50–200 entries, each with a category and a golden-set test reference), schema-validated in CI; every change goes through a deploy and the golden-set tests (SPEC §7).
- 2026-09-19: A site key moves from `draft` to `live` only through a server-side check (at least one contact method and one allowed origin); draft keys work from allowed origins with a test-mode badge (SPEC §4.1, §6.3).
- 2026-09-19: CI runs a Postgres service container so the Alembic upgrade-head test (1.1.h) runs in CI from day one; that test is an integration test reading `TEST_DATABASE_URL`, skipped locally with an explicit message when unset, and must fail (not skip) when `CI=true` and the var is missing. Qdrant is not needed in CI yet (Step 1.1).

- 2026-09-19: Step 1.1.c — Settings (backend/app/config.py) reads only the process environment (no env_file), is frozen, and is served through get_settings() (functools.lru_cache). DATABASE_URL is a SecretStr, validated to require the `postgresql+psycopg` scheme; QDRANT_URL is validated to require http/https. Verified against the installed pydantic 2.13.5 / pydantic-settings 2.15.0: `hide_input_in_errors=True` hides a bad value from str()/repr() of a ValidationError but not from the structured exc.errors() list, which still carries the raw input. Frozen-instance assignment raises pydantic_core.ValidationError, not TypeError.
- 2026-09-19: Duplication check run after 1.1.c (nothing to consolidate).
- 2026-09-19: Startup code reads settings only via get_settings(), which raises SettingsError with input-free messages; never log exc.errors().
- 2026-09-19: DATABASE_URL must include scheme postgresql+psycopg, a hostname and a database name.
- 2026-09-19: Guard test enforces that only get_settings() constructs settings, .errors() is never called, and database_url_str() has an explicit allow-list of callers.
- 2026-09-20: Product name: SamoFlow (renamed from VileSite on 2026-09-20; brand name only; code, packages and images keep the neutral name widgetplatform until a rename task is scheduled).
- 2026-09-20: Step 1.1.d — `.env.example` declares exactly the five variables Settings requires (no more, no less); required names are derived from `Settings.model_fields` in the test plus an `EXTRA_EXAMPLE_KEYS` constant (empty tuple today) for any future deliberate addition. `API_HOST=0.0.0.0` is the container-listen placeholder; `DATABASE_URL`'s password placeholder is exactly `change-me`, asserted by a dedicated test so a real secret pasted over it would be caught.
- 2026-09-20: Step 1.1.e — `create_app(settings: Settings | None = None)` in `backend/app/main.py` builds the FastAPI app; a `None` settings argument goes through `get_settings()` only (never `Settings()` directly, enforced by the existing config guard test since it scans every file under `backend/app`). Nothing at module level reads the environment. Title is the neutral `"widgetplatform API"`; version comes from `importlib.metadata.version("widgetplatform")` (works because Task 1.1.b's editable install makes the package resolvable). `/health` (backend/app/health.py) is liveness-only and touches neither Postgres nor Qdrant. Docs/redoc/openapi are disabled only when `app_env == "production"`. No CORS, middleware, database or Qdrant code, and no module-level `app` variable (Compose/Docker will run `uvicorn app.main:create_app --factory` from 1.1.g/1.1.i onward).
- 2026-09-20: Step 1.1.f — `backend/app/worker.py`'s `run(stop)` calls `get_settings()` only, logs one INFO line containing `app_env` only (never database_url/qdrant_url), then awaits the stop event; `main()` configures logging, registers SIGTERM/SIGINT handlers via `loop.add_signal_handler` that set the stop event, and exits 0 on clean shutdown or non-zero with no traceback on `SettingsError`. No job logic, no polling loop, no dependency on Postgres/Qdrant. Verified by hand: with the signal-handler registration removed, `SIGTERM`'s default OS disposition still terminates the (unhandled) process quickly rather than hanging it — the process exits with code -15 (terminated by signal), not 0, so the test still fails fast on `assert exit_code == 0` rather than by hitting the 10-second `wait_for` timeout.
- 2026-09-20: Duplication check and simplification review after 1.1.d/e/f: shared conftest.py, three redundant tests deleted, env_file guard added; dependency-set test to be removed when the lockfile task (1.1.o) lands.
- 2026-09-20: Step 1.1.h — Alembic skeleton generated with `alembic init` (default generic template, alembic==1.20.0) then trimmed: `alembic.ini` keeps `script_location = %(here)s/alembic` (the tool's own default), drops the noise comments and the unused `sqlalchemy.url` placeholder line (no database URL lives in the ini); the generated `README` is deleted; `alembic/versions/.gitkeep` keeps the empty directory in git (git does not track empty folders, and Alembic fails if the folder is missing). `alembic/env.py` gets its URL from `get_settings().database_url_str()` and builds the engine directly with `sqlalchemy.create_engine(url, poolclass=NullPool)`; it never calls `config.set_main_option` — verified by hand that pattern breaks with `ValueError: invalid interpolation syntax` on a literal "%" in the password, and would also copy the secret into the Config object. Offline mode is dropped (not "enough extra code to be free"): supporting it without `set_main_option` would need its own URL-plumbing, so only online mode exists; `target_metadata = None` with a `TODO(1.2)` marker (models arrive in 1.2). `backend/tests/test_config_guard.py` (Task 1.1.c's guard) now also scans `backend/alembic` and allow-lists `alembic/env.py` in `ALLOWED_DATABASE_URL_CALLERS` — verified by hand that a scratch file elsewhere calling `database_url_str()` still fails the guard.
- 2026-09-20: Step 1.1.h — Test database is Option A: integration tests read `TEST_DATABASE_URL` from the environment (never construct one from parts). `require_test_database()` (backend/tests/conftest.py) is the single guarded entry point: unset + `CI` != "true" → `pytest.skip` with the local `docker run` command; unset + `CI` == "true" → `pytest.fail` (CI must run this test, not silently skip it); set but not a `postgresql+psycopg` URL naming a database ending in `_test` → `pytest.fail`, never skip, and before anything is touched — this is the safety rail that keeps the test's `DROP SCHEMA public CASCADE` off a real database. `backend/tests/test_alembic.py`'s integration test resets the schema through a sync SQLAlchemy engine, then runs `python -m alembic upgrade head` as a subprocess (harness env: `APP_ENV=test`, `QDRANT_URL`/`API_HOST`/`API_PORT` harmless, `DATABASE_URL` = the test URL) twice, asserting exit 0 both times, that the public schema then contains exactly `alembic_version` (Alembic does create it with zero revisions), and that neither run's captured stdout/stderr contains the real password.
- 2026-09-20: Step 1.1.h — Postgres tag for the local/CI test database: `postgres:18.2-trixie`, verified to exist with `docker manifest inspect` (18.2 is the newest patch of the newest stable major, 18, published for Debian trixie as of 2026-09-20; 19.0 and 18.3 do not exist yet).
- 2026-09-20: Step 1.1.h — `backend/Dockerfile`'s final stage now also copies `alembic.ini` (`COPY --chmod=0644`) and `alembic/` (`COPY --chmod=0755`, so the directory stays traversable) alongside the existing `entrypoint.sh` copy — root-owned, not writable by the app user (verified by hand: `touch` as uid 10001 fails with Permission denied on both). `entrypoint.sh` gained a third mode, `migrate`, that runs `exec alembic upgrade head`; it does not check `API_HOST`/`API_PORT` itself, since `migrate` never uses them and `get_settings()` still validates all five required variables when `alembic/env.py` calls it. Usage message updated to `{api|worker|migrate}`.
- 2026-09-20: Step 1.1.h ruff — `backend/tests/test_alembic.py`'s `subprocess.run` call needed one inline `# noqa: S603` (bandit's "check for execution of untrusted input"): its argument list is fixed test-internal strings (`sys.executable`, `"-m"`, `"alembic"`, `"upgrade"`/`"head"`/`"history"`), not user input. No per-file-ignore was needed in `pyproject.toml`, and the template `env.py` (once rewritten per the design above) passes lint with no ignores at all.
- 2026-09-20: Step 1.1.h refinement (post-review) — `backend/tests/test_alembic.py`'s `_reset_public_schema`/`_table_names` take a pre-built SQLAlchemy `Engine`, never a raw URL string, and wrap their connect/execute calls in `except SQLAlchemyError` that re-raises a plain `RuntimeError` naming only `engine.url.host`/`engine.url.database` and the exception class, via `raise ... from None`; a `_test_engine` fixture builds the real `Engine` so the raw password is a local only in the fixture's own frame, which is never part of a test-body failure's traceback. A dedicated test (`test_reset_schema_failure_never_reveals_the_password_in_a_rendered_traceback`) proved this: it failed against the old code (the password appeared, both via psycopg's own internal frame locals and via the test's own local variable) and passes against the new code, checked with `traceback.TracebackException(..., capture_locals=True)` — strictly more revealing than pytest's default rendering.
- 2026-09-20: Step 1.1.h refinement — `_run_alembic`'s subprocess environment is now built explicitly (`_subprocess_env()`): the five `Settings` variables plus `PATH`/`HOME` from the parent, nothing else; a stray `PGPASSWORD`/`PGHOST`/`PGUSER` in the developer's or CI's shell can no longer reach the child process. Verified with a unit test that sets those three and asserts they're absent from the built env dict.
- 2026-09-20: Step 1.1.h refinement — `test_env_py_compiles_cleanly` (`py_compile.compile(doraise=True)`) added: `alembic history` never calls `env.py`'s `run_env()` (confirmed by reading Alembic's own `command.history` source — it only runs the environment when `revision_environment` is set or `--indicate-current` is passed, neither true here), so without this test a syntax error in `env.py` would pass every check on the common no-database path. The `alembic history` test's docstring now says only what it proves (ini + versions/ directory valid), not that it exercises `env.py`.
- 2026-09-20: Step 1.1.h refinement — `alembic/env.py` catches `SettingsError` from `get_settings()` and prints `str(exc)` to stderr with `sys.exit(1)`, mirroring `app/worker.py`'s `main()`; a subprocess test with an empty environment asserts a non-zero exit, every missing field named, and no `"Traceback"` in stderr.
- 2026-09-20: Step 1.1.h refinement — `require_test_database()` gained a host guard after the existing scheme/suffix check: the URL's host must be `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, or listed (comma-separated, case-insensitive) in `TEST_DATABASE_ALLOWED_HOSTS`; otherwise `pytest.fail` with a message naming only the (non-secret) hostname. This closes the gap the 1.1.h review found: a `..._test`-named database on an arbitrary reachable host (e.g. a shared/staging server) previously passed the guard untouched — verified with a unit test reproducing that exact example (`prod-db.internal.example.com` + `legacy_customer_test`), which now fails.
- 2026-09-20: Step 1.1.h refinement — `app/config.py`'s `_POSTGRES_SCHEME` renamed to the public `POSTGRES_SCHEME` (no behavior change, two use sites, both in the same file); `backend/tests/conftest.py` imports it instead of duplicating the `"postgresql+psycopg"` literal.
- 2026-09-20: Step 1.1.g — base image pinned to `python:3.12.14-slim-trixie` (Debian trixie is the current stable Debian release; 3.12.14 is the latest 3.12 patch published for it as of 2026-09-20); existence verified with `docker manifest inspect python:3.12.14-slim-trixie` before use (returned a valid multi-arch manifest list). `psycopg[binary]` kept for v1 instead of building from source against the system libpq: the container is rebuilt on every deploy to pick up base-image patches, and the vulnerability scan (1.1.o) will cover the bundled libpq/OpenSSL; revisit in Phase 7 if that scan flags them. `uvicorn --no-server-header` confirmed supported by the pinned uvicorn==0.53.0 (`uvicorn --help` lists `--server-header / --no-server-header`) and used in entrypoint.sh's `api` mode so the `server:` response header is suppressed. Non-root numeric UID/GID 10001 (no login shell, no home dir), WORKDIR /app, no HEALTHCHECK, no .dockerignore yet (1.1.n).
- 2026-09-20: Step 1.1.g refinement — multi-stage build: a builder stage (pip + hatchling, both build-time only) installs the package into a venv at /venv; the final stage is the same pinned base image, copying in only /venv and entrypoint.sh, with `pip uninstall --yes pip` run against /venv at the end of the builder stage so the app venv ships no pip (verified: `python -m pip --version` in the built image fails with `No module named pip`) — CORRECTED 2026-09-25 (Step 1.1.o.d): this covered only the venv; the base image's own system Python still shipped a fully working pip (see the 1.1.m decision below and the 1.1.o.d decision further down for the fix). File modes come from `COPY --chmod=0755 entrypoint.sh ...` plus the builder's own root-owned-by-default output for /venv (both COPY instructions run before `USER 10001`), which is simpler than a separate `chmod`/`find` pass and gives the same result: /app and /venv are root-owned and not writable by the app user (verified by hand: `touch` inside either as uid 10001 fails with Permission denied). The `find __pycache__`/`*.pyc` cleanup step is removed — it only mattered when app/ was copied straight into the shipped image; now app/ is copied only into the discarded builder stage, and pip's wheel build already excludes bytecode. The Dockerfile documents UID 10001 as a fixed convention (not host-assigned), so file ownership stays stable across rebuilds and hosts; Compose (1.1.i) must reuse this same UID wherever it sets `user:` or touches ownership of files from this image. Image content size changed only marginally (63,312,007 → 62,831,945 bytes) since the prior single-stage build had no apt build tooling to strip — the main gain is removing pip/hatchling from the runtime image, not size. Entrypoint refinement: `api` mode now checks `API_HOST`/`API_PORT` itself (`[ -z ... ]` before the `exec`) and prints one `entrypoint.sh: <VAR> is required for mode 'api'` line to stderr with exit 2, instead of surfacing `set -u`'s raw "parameter not set" shell error; the script does not duplicate Settings' own validation (scheme checks, etc.) — only the two variables it uses itself.

Verified: `ruff check --show-settings` now reports an identical enabled-rule-code list and `line_length = 100` for `evals/run.py` and a `backend/` file; `ruff check backend evals` (repo root) and `cd backend && ruff check .` both pass; the `tests/*` → S101 per-file-ignore still resolves only against `backend/tests/*` (its `absolute_matcher` is anchored under `backend/pyproject.toml`'s own directory) regardless of which directory ruff is invoked from. Effectiveness proof (scratch file added under `evals/`, then removed; real `run.py`/`README.md` untouched): a file with `PASSWORD = "hunter2"` and a bare `assert` passed cleanly under ruff's bare defaults in an isolated directory with no config, but is flagged (S105, S101) once placed under `evals/` in this repo with the fix applied.
- 2026-09-20: Step 1.1.j — Makefile (repo root), plain GNU Make with POSIX shell recipes, `COMPOSE = docker compose --env-file .env -f deploy/docker-compose.yml`, default goal `help`. Targets and what each does: `help` (one line per target, plain `echo`); `env` (creates `.env` from `.env.example` only if `.env` does not already exist — never overwrites; every target touching `$(COMPOSE)` depends on it); `up` (`env`, then `$(COMPOSE) up -d --build`); `down` (`$(COMPOSE) down`, keeps volumes); `down-volumes` (`$(COMPOSE) down -v`, prints a destroys-data warning first, kept as a separate target from `down` so volume loss is never a flag away from an ordinary teardown); `migrate` (`env`, then `$(COMPOSE) run --rm migrate` — runs in Docker, so it never depends on the caller's working directory or local Python environment); `test` (`cd backend && python -m pytest tests -q`, no database needed — the Alembic integration test skips itself); `test-db` (`env`, then `$(COMPOSE) --profile test up -d test-db`, then a `pg_isready`-polling loop, 30 x 1s, failing with a clear message on timeout); `test-db-down` (`$(COMPOSE) --profile test stop test-db` then `rm -f -s -v test-db`); `test-all` (starts `test-db`, then one shell recipe that runs the suite from `backend/` with `TEST_DATABASE_URL` set only for that pytest invocation, captures its exit code explicitly, always calls `test-db-down`, then exits with the captured code — cleanup and the tests' real exit status never depend on make's own error handling); `lint` (`ruff check backend evals` from the repo root); `evals` (`python evals/run.py` from the repo root). Every recipe fails on first non-zero exit (no leading `-`, no `|| true`).
- 2026-09-20: Step 1.1.j implementation notes — `docker compose run/up/exec/stop/rm` all work on an explicitly-named service without its profile being activated (verified by hand: `run --rm migrate` and `up -d test-db`/`exec`/`stop`/`rm` on `test-db` all succeeded without `--profile`); `--profile test` is still passed on the `test-db` calls to match the convention `deploy/docker-compose.yml`'s own header comment already established in 1.1.i, `--profile tools` is not added to `migrate` for the same reason (that convention omits it). `test-all`'s recipe wraps its `cd backend && ... pytest` step in a subshell `(...)`: without it, the `cd` would still be in effect for the rest of the same shell recipe, and the following `$(MAKE) test-db-down` call would then resolve `deploy/docker-compose.yml` relative to `backend/` instead of the repo root.

Only `actions/checkout` and `actions/setup-python` are used, each pinned to a full 40-character commit SHA with a trailing version comment: `actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7.0.1` and `actions/setup-python@5fda3b95a4ea91299a34e894583c3862153e4b97 # v7.0.0` — both the current stable major (v7); verified via `git ls-remote --tags` against each repo (confirming the tag→SHA mapping) cross-checked against each repo's GitHub Releases API (confirming publish dates and that no newer patch exists) on 2026-09-20. `checkout` uses `persist-credentials: false`; `setup-python` uses `python-version: "3.12"`, `cache: pip`, `cache-dependency-path: backend/pyproject.toml` — CORRECTED 2026-09-25 (Step 1.1.o.e): changed to `backend/requirements-dev.lock`, the file that actually determines what gets installed once `make install` replaces the plain `pip install -e` step; see the 1.1.o.e decision further down.
- 2026-09-20: Step 1.1.m — deviation from the older open-marker notes (approved in-session): job `test` does not use a native GitHub Actions Postgres service container. It installs the backend package (`pip install -e "./backend[dev]"`), runs `make lint`, then `make test-all` (Task 1.1.j), which starts `deploy/docker-compose.yml`'s `test-db` profile — the same pinned Postgres tag used by local development — and always tears it down. This is the single source of truth for the CI test database: there is no second place a Postgres version could drift. GitHub Actions runners set `CI=true` by default, which is what makes `backend/tests/conftest.py`'s `require_test_database()` fail (not skip) the Alembic integration test if `TEST_DATABASE_URL` is ever unexpectedly missing. `TEST_DATABASE_ALLOWED_HOSTS` is not needed: `test-db` is published on `127.0.0.1`, already in `require_test_database()`'s default allowed hosts.

A `trap ... EXIT` always removes every container and temp file it started. Checks, each printing one PASS/FAIL line: (a) `docker run --entrypoint id` is uid `10001`; (b) `python -m pip --version` fails inside the app venv (system Python's own separate pip is out of scope — see the new open marker below); (c) a bogus mode exits 2 and prints the usage line; (d) `api` mode published on `127.0.0.1:18080` answers `GET /health` within 30s with `{"status":"ok"}` and no `server:` header, then `docker stop` exits 0 within 10s; (e) `worker` mode logs `"worker starting: app_env=development"` within 30s, then stops the same way; (f) `migrate` mode against `invalid.invalid` (RFC 2606, never resolves — fails fast on DNS rather than a TCP-connect timeout; `PGCONNECT_TIMEOUT=5` bounds the worst case) exits non-zero without the password `change-me` appearing in its output. Proved each of the 11 rules can fail, in a scratch copy of the file text (the real file was never touched): this caught two real gaps in the first draft — the SHA-pin check silently passed a non-hex ref like `@v7` because the matching regex required hex characters immediately after `@` and simply didn't match otherwise; and the `make test-all`/`make lint` checks searched the whole job block's text, so removing the real `run: make test-all` line still passed because the step's own explanatory comment above it also contained the phrase "make test-all". - 2026-09-20: Step 1.1.o tool choices approved (rule 8) — uv (build-time only, never in the runtime image), pip-audit, Grype via `anchore/scan-action`, GitHub Dependabot (config only). Rationale: pip's own native lock support and `pip install --pylock.toml` are still experimental and platform-specific, while `uv pip compile --universal` produces one cross-platform hashed lock; the Trivy scanner ecosystem was compromised on 2026-03-19 (a malicious `v0.69.4` release and force-pushed `trivy-action` tags), which is why Grype was chosen instead; every third-party GitHub Action stays pinned by full commit SHA, the same convention 1.1.m already established.

- 2026-09-20: Step 1.1.o Dependabot verification — the native `uv` package-ecosystem regenerates locks only for a real `uv.lock` project or a `name.in`→`name.txt` pip-compile-style pair; `backend/requirements.lock`/`requirements-dev.lock` (compiled directly from `pyproject.toml`, no `.in` source, no `[tool.uv]` project) match neither layout, and the plain `pip` ecosystem edits a version pin without regenerating hashes — so **no Python package-ecosystem entry is configured** for backend's lockfiles (1.1.o.g). Multiple open `dependabot-core` issues (#2057, #4597, #13027) confirm the `docker` ecosystem does not resolve `ARG`-substituted `FROM` lines, so it cannot see or bump `backend/Dockerfile`'s `ARG PYTHON_IMAGE=...` / `FROM ${PYTHON_IMAGE}` base image. `deploy/docker-compose.yml` needs the separate `docker-compose` package-ecosystem value (general availability since February 2025) — `docker` only parses Dockerfile `FROM` lines, not Compose `image:` properties.
- 2026-09-20: Step 1.1.o lockfile file names — GitHub's dependency-graph documentation (`dependency-graph-supported-package-ecosystems`; the pip table row was fetched and inspected directly, not summarized from memory) recognizes only `requirements.txt` and `pipfile.lock` as pip's "Recommended files", with `pipfile`/`setup.py` as "Additional files" — no wildcard pattern and no documented `requirements-dev.txt` (or any other dev-variant) name. Since a recognized name exists for the runtime lockfile but not for the dev/superset one, both files keep the `.lock` extension (`backend/requirements.lock`, `backend/requirements-dev.lock`) rather than mixing `.txt`/`.lock` naming, per the user's own fallback instruction ("otherwise keep the .lock names").
- 2026-09-20: Step 1.1.o — `make lock` (1.1.o.c) regenerates both lockfiles without `--upgrade`, so `uv pip compile` treats the currently committed pins as preferences and leaves them unchanged when nothing upstream has changed (to be proven for real by running `make lock` twice and diffing the output, byte-for-byte). `make lock-upgrade` runs the same compile with `--upgrade` to intentionally pull newer versions; this is the command the new manual maintenance routine (§6 Project constraints) runs.
- 2026-09-20: Step 1.1.o residual risk, owner Phase 7 — `pip install --require-hashes` (implied automatically by any already-hashed requirements file) does not extend to `[build-system] requires` packages: by default, `pip install --no-deps .` (and `pip install -e .`) builds in an isolated environment that downloads the build backend (hatchling) and its own transitive dependencies with no hash verification, regardless of `--require-hashes` on the main install. Confirmed empirically against the installed pip 26.2.1: `pip install --help`'s `--no-build-isolation` text reads "Build dependencies specified by PEP 518 must be already installed if this option is used" (i.e. isolation is on, and those build dependencies are fetched fresh and unpinned, by default); this matches a filed, still-open pip issue describing exactly this gap as an undocumented bypass (`pypa/pip#13984`). No discrepancy from how the user described the risk. 1.1.o does not add `--no-build-isolation` or wheel-building machinery (that would require independently pinning and hash-verifying hatchling's own dependency tree — out of scope here). Accepted as a residual risk for now because: hatchling is already pinned to an exact version in `[build-system] requires` (not a floating range); the runtime image ships no build tools or pip at all once 1.1.o.d lands, so a compromised build backend cannot act again after the image is built, only during it; and CI has no secrets for a malicious build step to exfiltrate.

- 2026-09-25: Step 1.1.o.d — base-image finding (`python:3.12.14-slim-trixie`), inspected directly with `docker run`: the system Python's `pip` (25.0.1) is not a dpkg package (`dpkg -l | grep pip` empty) — the official image bootstraps it via `ensurepip` at image-build time, and the official image never removes it (no documented removal method). A clean `python3 -m pip uninstall --yes pip` (the same technique already used for `/venv` in 1.1.g) does fully remove it (`python -m pip --version` → `No module named pip`), but this alone is **not sufficient**: `ensurepip` keeps its own bundled wheel (`/usr/local/lib/python3.12/ensurepip/_bundled/pip-25.0.1-py3-none-any.whl`, 1.8MB, fully offline), and running `python3 -m ensurepip` after the uninstall fully reinstates a working pip with one command, no network needed — verified by hand. Per the task's own stop condition, this was reported and a decision asked for before proceeding; approved fix: also `rm -rf` ensurepip's directory (located via `sysconfig.get_path("stdlib")`, not a hard-coded `python3.12` path, so it keeps working if `PYTHON_IMAGE` is ever bumped to a different Python minor version). Verified after the fix: `python3 -m pip --version` and `python3 -m ensurepip --version` both fail with `No module named ...`; `python3` itself and `python3 -m venv` (needed at build time only, not at runtime) still behave correctly (venv creation fails gracefully without ensurepip, no interpreter crash). No `setuptools`/`wheel` were present as separate packages (`pip list` showed only `pip`), so nothing else needed removing. `backend/pyproject.toml`'s old "pip removed" 1.1.g decision line (above) was corrected in place to say it covered only the venv, not the system Python.

- 2026-09-25: Step 1.1.o.c — deviation (approved in-session): `backend/requirements.lock`/`backend/requirements-dev.lock` were regenerated with `--no-header` added (otherwise identical `uv pip compile` invocations from 1.1.o.b), so both files now start directly with the first package entry. The alternative (keep headers, strip the first 2 lines from both sides before diffing via temp files) would have kept the header but pushed `lock-check`'s recipe to ~9 lines instead of ~8; the user chose dropping headers everywhere instead, which is why this task touches the two lockfiles despite the stated file scope being Makefile-only. No package, version or hash content changed — verified with `git diff` (2-line deletion in each file, nothing else) and by re-running `backend/tests/test_pyproject.py`'s lockfile-consistency tests (still 9/9 passed; they never read the header). `make lock` re-verified idempotent afterwards (byte-identical across two runs, freshly checked against a clean snapshot after the header change, not reused from the pre-change idempotency check).

Built a standalone script, `hooks.py` (repo root, same placement precedent as `evals/run.py`): plain-text/regex line matching (no AST, per the task's own explicit design option), word-boundary matching to avoid substring false positives; TODO grouped by parsed `TODO(<step>)` step number (numeric-tuple sort, graceful string fallback for malformed/placeholder steps); ASSUMPTION/UNCERTAIN/NOTE have no step concept (rule 4) so are grouped by marker name instead; skips `.venv*`/`__pycache__`/`.pytest_cache`/`.ruff_cache`. - 2026-09-25: Step 1.1.o.g second follow-up — added a **temporary** `ignore: [{dependency-name: "postgres"}]` rule to `.github/dependabot.yml`'s `docker-compose` ecosystem entry, silencing the known Dependabot YAML-anchor/alias file-updater bug (documented in gap 3, confirmed 2026-09-25 — see the entry below) so its weekly/change-triggered scan stops failing on every postgres update attempt. This explicitly qualifies, but does not reverse, the earlier follow-up's rejection of an `ignore:` workaround "as strictly worse than doing nothing" (losing the "a newer tag exists" log signal): that tradeoff is accepted now, on the understanding that this is a silencing of a known bug, not a decision to stop tracking Postgres updates, and must be removed once any of: (1) dependabot-core fixes the anchor-handling bug, (2) the `x-postgres-image` anchor is manually removed from `deploy/docker-compose.yml`, or (3) the next scheduled review of Project constraints' manual image-tag routine — whichever comes first. Scoped narrowly by `dependency-name: "postgres"` only, so `qdrant/qdrant` and `widgetplatform-backend` in the same ecosystem entry continue to update normally — verified by re-parsing the file (`pyyaml`) and confirming only the `docker-compose` entry gained the new `ignore:` key. Gap 3's own comment block is **not** changed and does not claim the bug is resolved — it is only silenced; the underlying dependabot-core bug remains unfixed. `backend/tests/test_dependabot_guard.py` gained `test_dependabot_yml_ignores_postgres_scoped_by_dependency_name_only`, asserting every `ignore:` rule has a `dependency-name` field (so a future edit can't accidentally widen it into ignoring the whole ecosystem entry) and that one of them is scoped exactly to `"postgres"` — proved able to fail on scratch-edited text (a rule with the `dependency-name` key stripped out, leaving a bare/unscoped rule that would ignore every docker-compose dependency), real file confirmed untouched throughout. Verified: `make lint` clean; the file still parses as valid YAML with the intended structure; full `backend/tests` without a test database: 117 passed/1 skipped (up from 116 — the one new test).
- 2026-09-25: Step 1.1.o.g follow-up — Dependabot's own docker-compose update-scan failed on `postgres` (`deploy/docker-compose.yml`, trying to bump the shared `x-postgres-image` anchor from `18.2-trixie` to `18.4-trixie`) with a Ruby `RuntimeError: Expected content to change!` inside dependabot-core's own updater — not a CI or security-workflow failure. Root-caused directly from dependabot-core's source at the exact commit the failed run used (`283ae3cebfc79b269de0d7a6d41ddde2edfde682`, a real, same-day commit): `docker_compose/file_parser.rb` parses with `YAML.safe_load(..., aliases: true)`, so it correctly resolves the `*postgres-image` alias and detects the newer tag; but `docker_compose/file_updater.rb`'s `updated_dependency_files` always calls `shared_file_updater.rb`'s `updated_dockerfile_content`, which never re-parses the YAML — it rewrites the raw file text with a regex requiring the literal tag immediately after `image:`. The literal tag `postgres:18.2-trixie` exists only on the anchor-definition line (preceded by `&postgres-image`, not `image:`); both service `image:` lines contain only the alias `*postgres-image`. The regex never matches, the rewrite is a no-op, and dependabot-core's own `raise "Expected content to change!"` fires — confirmed by reading the exact methods named in the traceback, not inferred. Searched dependabot-core's issue tracker thoroughly (the literal error string, "anchor", "alias", "merge key", each combined with docker-compose/docker_compose) and found no existing issue describing this exact anchor+alias scenario; the closest related reports (#16234, #9653, #13351, #15100) are different bugs/feature-requests in the same fragile code area, individually checked and ruled out. Confirmed `qdrant` is unaffected: its `image: qdrant/qdrant:v1.19.1` is a plain inline string with no anchor (only one consumer, so 1.1.i never needed one there) — only `postgres` (shared by `postgres`+`test-db`) uses the anchor pattern. Added a third gap comment block to `.github/dependabot.yml`, matching the style of the existing two, recording all of the above. No config-level fix exists (this is dependabot-core's own update-rewriting code, not a `dependabot.yml` option); the only config-level workaround would be an `ignore:` rule for `postgres`, which was rejected as strictly worse than doing nothing — it would stop Dependabot from even detecting and reporting a newer tag exists (today it still correctly logs "Latest version is 18.4-trixie" before failing), losing that signal entirely. Unwinding the anchor/alias pattern to appease Dependabot was also rejected: it exists as the single source of truth keeping `postgres` and `test-db` on the same tag (1.1.i's decisions), and that reason still holds. The existing manual routine's wording (PROJECT_SPEC.md's Project constraints, "Manual dependency/image maintenance routine (Step 1.1.o)") already names "Postgres" explicitly, so no wording change was needed there. Verified: the file still parses as valid YAML with the same structure (checked via a `python:3.12-slim` container with `pyyaml`); `make lint` clean; full `backend/tests` without a test database: 115 passed/1 skipped, unchanged (no test logic touched — the existing `test_dependabot_yml_explains_the_dockerfile_arg_gap` check still passes since both `ARG` and `PYTHON_IMAGE` remain in the file).
- 2026-09-25: Step 1.1.o.g — new `.github/dependabot.yml`: two `updates` entries, `github-actions` (`directory: "/"`, Dependabot's documented convention that searches `.github/workflows` automatically) and `docker-compose` (`directory: "/deploy"`, where `docker-compose.yml` actually lives), both `schedule.interval: "weekly"`, `open-pull-requests-limit: 5`, and a `groups` block (`patterns: ["*"]`) so each ecosystem's updates land in one PR instead of one per dependency. No auto-merge configured (dependabot.yml has no such key; a separate workflow would be needed and none was added — every PR needs human review). All of this re-verified today against GitHub's live docs (fetched and parsed directly, not recalled from memory): `docker-compose` and `github-actions` are confirmed current `package-ecosystem` values, the `groups`/`patterns`/`open-pull-requests-limit`/`schedule.interval` syntax confirmed against the current configuration-options reference page, and the `directory` leading-slash convention (`"/deploy"`, not `"deploy"`) confirmed against a live example. Two deliberate gaps, both documented with a comment in the file itself pointing at the manual `make lock-upgrade` routine already recorded in PROJECT_SPEC.md's Project constraints: (1) no Python/pip/uv package-ecosystem entry, per the existing 2026-09-20 finding (neither ecosystem safely regenerates backend's hash-pinned lockfiles); (2) no `docker` ecosystem entry for `backend/`, re-verified today directly against dependabot-core's own issue tracker rather than just docs — issue #2057 ("Add global ARG support for Dockerfiles") is still open, last updated 2026-08-04, and describes exactly `backend/Dockerfile`'s pattern (`ARG PYTHON_IMAGE=...` then `FROM ${PYTHON_IMAGE}`); the two previously-cited issues that are now closed were checked individually and found unrelated to this gap (#4597 closed as "not planned" — the ARG-FROM feature request itself was declined, not implemented; #13027 closed as a resolved support question about registry-hostname resolution, a different problem). `backend/tests/test_dependabot_guard.py` (new, matching the project's per-config-file guard-test convention) asserts: the file exists with basic top-level `version:`/`updates:` structure and `- package-ecosystem:`-shaped list items (no YAML-parser dependency); both `github-actions` and `docker-compose` entries are present; no `pip`/`uv` package-ecosystem entry exists anywhere; a comment mentioning both `ARG` and `PYTHON_IMAGE` exists, so the Dockerfile-gap explanation can't be silently deleted. Verified: the file parses as valid YAML (checked via a `python:3.12-slim` container with `pyyaml`, since no YAML-parsing dependency exists locally or in the project) with exactly the intended structure; a scratch-sabotaged copy (docker-compose entry changed to `"pip"`) confirmed the no-python-ecosystem check actually catches it; `make lint` clean; full `backend/tests` without a test database: 115 passed/1 skipped (up from 111 — the 4 new guard tests).
- 2026-09-25: Step 1.1.o.f second follow-up — root-caused why `.grype.yaml` did not suppress `CVE-2026-82049` in the real CI run despite being committed and passing local verification. Traced the full call chain from source (all fetched at the exact pinned/matching commits, not recalled from memory): `anchore/scan-action`'s `action.js`/`dist/index.js` at the pinned commit only pushes a grype `-c <path>` flag when its own `config` input is non-empty — `security.yml` never set that input, so grype was left to its own auto-detection. Traced grype v0.119.0's actual config-search code (`cmd/grype/cli/cli.go` → `anchore/clio@v0.1.1`'s `setup_config.go` → `anchore/fangs@v0.1.1`'s `finders.go`/`config.go`, dereferencing each release tag to its real commit via the GitHub API where needed): the first and default finder, `FindInCwd`, opens the literal relative path `./.grype.yaml` — resolved against the grype process's actual working directory at the moment it runs, nothing else. Traced `actions/runner`'s `NodeScriptActionHandler.cs` (the code that launches a JS action like this one): absent an override, it launches with `workingDirectory = GetGitHubContext("workspace")`, i.e. `$GITHUB_WORKSPACE` — which is also where `actions/checkout` places the repo by default. On paper, this chain should have found `.grype.yaml` at the repo root. It plainly didn't in the real run, and the local Docker-based checks done for the first follow-up entry (mounting the repo as the container's cwd) couldn't have caught the gap, because they only ever exercised the CWD-based auto-detection path working correctly — they never tested the failure mode itself. Found the real fix by testing that failure mode directly: ran `grype` with its working directory deliberately pointed somewhere with no `.grype.yaml` (`/somewhere/else`, mounting the real repo separately at `/github/workspace`) — confirmed `CVE-2026-82049` reappears and the scan fails (exit 2) with no explicit `-c` flag, reproducing the real CI symptom exactly; then added `-c /github/workspace/.grype.yaml` (an absolute path) to the same invocation and confirmed it correctly suppresses only that one CVE (exit 0), regardless of the mismatched working directory. Also found, by reading `fangs`'s `load.go`, that an explicit config path is *not* automatically safe either: `findConfigurationFiles` resolves a *relative* `-c` path the same CWD-dependent way, so `config: .grype.yaml` (relative) would have carried the identical risk — the fix must be an *absolute* path. Fixed `.github/workflows/security.yml`'s `image-scan` job: the `anchore/scan-action` step now sets `config: ${{ github.workspace }}/.grype.yaml` (GitHub's own documented absolute-path context expression, interpolated by the runner before the action even executes, independent of whatever the action's actual working directory turns out to be) — with a comment recording why. Added a matching regression check to `backend/tests/test_ci_guard.py`'s `_security_specific_violations`: the `anchore/scan-action` step's block must contain the literal `config: ${{ github.workspace }}/.grype.yaml` string, not just any `config:` line. Verified end to end: `actionlint` clean; `docker build` + `anchore/grype:v0.119.0 ... -c /repo/.grype.yaml` from the repo root (the standard case) still suppresses exactly `CVE-2026-82049` with the other 9 findings untouched, exit 0; `test_ci_guard.py` (3/3) passes against the real, updated file; `make lint` clean; full `backend/tests` without a test database: 111 passed/1 skipped (unaffected — no Python logic changed beyond the one new guard assertion).
- 2026-09-25: Step 1.1.o.f follow-up — the real GitHub Actions run of `image-scan` found one finding: `CVE-2026-82049` in the base image's own `python` package (3.12.14, type `binary`), severity High (a `tarfile` extraction flaw: a crafted archive with a hard link to a symlink can write outside the destination directory during extraction). Investigated and confirmed: no 3.12.x/3.13.x patch release exists yet (the fix has only landed in the 3.14 development branch, 3.14.0b1) — there is nothing to upgrade to; the vulnerable code path is `tarfile` extraction of untrusted archives, and nothing in this image calls `tarfile` on untrusted input (`pip`, the most plausible caller, was already removed from both Python interpreters in 1.1.g/1.1.o.d). Added `.grype.yaml` at the repo root with one ignore rule scoped to the exact reported match (`vulnerability: CVE-2026-82049`, `package: {name: python, version: 3.12.14, type: binary}`) plus a `reason:` field and a longer explanatory comment recording both facts above and that the rule is scoped to expire the moment the base image's Python version changes. Initially concluded `anchore/scan-action` needs no explicit `config:` input — CORRECTED below (2026-09-25, same day): the real Actions run still failed with `CVE-2026-82049` even with `.grype.yaml` committed, because auto-detection could not be relied on after all; see the follow-up entry for the actual root cause and fix (an explicit, absolute `config:` input). The local verification recorded here (mounting the repo root as the container's cwd and confirming suppression) was real but not sufficiently faithful to the GitHub Actions execution environment to catch the gap — see the follow-up entry for a verification method proven to actually distinguish the two cases. `actionlint` clean against `security.yml`; `make lint` and the full `backend/tests` without a test database unaffected (111 passed, 1 skipped — this change touched no Python code or test logic).
- 2026-09-25: Step 1.1.o.f — new `.github/workflows/security.yml`: jobs `python-audit` (checkout, setup-python matching ci.yml's pin and its `cache-dependency-path: backend/requirements-dev.lock` pattern, `make install`, `make audit` — pip-audit's own default non-zero-on-any-finding exit, not softened) and `image-scan` (checkout, `docker build -t widgetplatform-backend:scan backend`, then `anchore/scan-action` with `fail-build: true`, `severity-cutoff: high`, `only-fixed: true`, `grype-version: v0.119.0`). Triggers: `schedule` (Monday 03:17 UTC — start of the work week so a finding is fresh Monday morning; a non-zero minute avoids the top-of-hour congestion GitHub's docs warn scheduled workflows about), `workflow_dispatch`, and `push`/`pull_request` restricted to a `paths:` filter on the six files that can change what gets audited/scanned (`backend/pyproject.toml`, `backend/requirements.lock`, `backend/requirements-dev.lock`, `backend/Dockerfile`, `deploy/docker-compose.yml`, `.github/workflows/security.yml`). Same fork-PR safety as ci.yml: `contents: read` only, no `security-events: write` (SARIF upload is a possible future upgrade, out of scope here), never `pull_request_target`, `persist-credentials: false` on every checkout, `actions/checkout@3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1 # v7.0.1` and `actions/setup-python@5fda3b95a4ea91299a34e894583c3862153e4b97 # v7.0.0` reused from ci.yml and re-verified still current (`git ls-remote --tags` against each repo, cross-checked with each repo's Releases API). `anchore/scan-action`: the SHA recorded in the 1.1.o planning phase (`8576240a41341d866dbd7ebc72cd9fc0ef023f54`) turned out to be **wrong** — it is the annotated tag object's own SHA, not the commit it points to; `uses:` must reference a commit, and the GitHub API rejected it ("SHA must identify a commit or a tree"). Re-verified for real this time: `git ls-remote --tags` shows `refs/tags/v7.4.2^{}` (the dereferenced/peeled commit) is `27805bf3b4e84b4a5c980df22ed233c00390a439`; confirmed this is a real commit via the Git Trees API, confirmed `v7.4.2` is still the latest non-prerelease release via the GitHub Releases API (published 2026-08-28), and fetched the actual `action.yml` at that commit to confirm the real input names (`image`, `fail-build`, `severity-cutoff`, `only-fixed`, `grype-version`, plus `output-format`/`output-file`/others not used here). Grype: confirmed `v0.119.0` is still the current latest release (GitHub Releases API, published 2026-09-17, unchanged since the 1.1.o planning check) and still newer than the action's own bundled default (`GrypeVersion.js` at the same commit: `v0.118.0`), so pinning it explicitly is still meaningful. The workflow guard (`backend/tests/test_ci_guard.py`) was generalized as pre-approved in the 1.1.o.f planning note: `_generic_workflow_violations(text)` now holds every check that must apply to *any* workflow file (permissions, no `pull_request_target`, no `secrets.`, every `uses:` SHA-pinned, no `latest` runner, every job has a timeout, every checkout sets `persist-credentials: false` — this last one moved here from being ci.yml-only) and is run once per file discovered under `.github/workflows/` via a new `test_every_workflow_file_passes_generic_checks`; `_ci_specific_violations`/`test_ci_workflow` keeps exactly the install/lock-check/lint/test-all ordering and image-job build+smoke checks, scoped to ci.yml only; a new `_security_specific_violations`/`test_security_workflow` checks the paths filter lists all six expected files, the schedule/cron trigger exists, `python-audit` runs `make audit`, and `image-scan` runs a `docker build` plus an `anchore/scan-action` step with `fail-build: true`/`severity-cutoff: high`. No file besides `backend/tests/test_ci_guard.py` needed touching for the generalization. Both new checks proved able to fail on scratch-edited text (never the real file): removing `persist-credentials: false` from one checkout, and removing the `severity-cutoff` input. Verified: `actionlint` (Docker `rhysd/actionlint:latest`, resolved to v1.7.12, the current release per its own GitHub Releases API — no prior pinned usage found anywhere in this repo to match, so this is the first use of it here) is clean against both `security.yml` and `ci.yml`; `make install && make audit` locally still passes with 0 findings, matching 1.1.o.c; `docker build -t widgetplatform-backend:scan backend` succeeds; `make lint` clean; full `backend/tests` without a test database: 111 passed/1 skipped (unchanged — same test count in `test_ci_guard.py`, 3, just regrouped). The real `anchore/scan-action` Grype scan cannot be faithfully exercised locally (its own GitHub-Actions-hosted environment/caching) and was not attempted — that needs an actual Actions run, which the user triggers.
- 2026-09-25: Step 1.1.o.e — `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s job `test` now runs `make install` (hash-checked against `backend/requirements-dev.lock`, then the backend package with `--no-deps`) in place of `python -m pip install -e "./backend[dev]"`, and a new `make lock-check` step runs immediately after, before `make lint`/`make test-all`, so a stale/tampered lockfile fails CI fast rather than silently installing something that no longer matches `backend/pyproject.toml`. This closes the loop 1.1.o.a–1.1.o.d built: the lockfiles exist and are hash-checked (o.a/o.b), the Makefile can regenerate and verify them (o.c), the Docker image installs from them (o.d), and now CI does too, with the same drift gate available locally via `make lock-check`. `backend/tests/test_ci_guard.py`'s `_check()` now requires `make install`, `make lock-check`, `make lint` and `make test-all` all present in job `test`, in that relative order (install first, since it puts `uv` on `PATH` for lock-check; lock-check before lint/test-all, to fail fast on drift) — proved able to fail two ways on scratch-edited text (never the real file): removing the `make lock-check` step, and swapping the `lock-check`/`lint` steps' order. `setup-python`'s `cache-dependency-path` was initially left as `backend/pyproject.toml` (out of the approved scope) and flagged as an open marker — then fixed in the same task, before pushing: changed to `backend/requirements-dev.lock`, the file that now actually determines what `make install` installs (`backend/pyproject.toml` is not read at install time — only `uv pip compile`/`lock-check` read it, and that's a separate cache from `setup-python`'s pip cache). Confirmed via the action's real `action.yml` at the pinned SHA that `cache-dependency-path` "supports wildcards or a list of file names", so a YAML list (`cache-dependency-path: |` with one path per line) was available, but a single file is the correct, smallest answer here since exactly one file determines the pip install. Verified locally, simulating CI exactly: in a clean Python 3.12.14 venv with nothing pre-installed beyond its own bootstrapped pip, ran `make install`, `make lock-check`, `make lint`, `make test-all` in that literal order (matching job `test`'s steps line for line) — each succeeded (`make test-all`: 112 passed). `make lint` from the normal dev environment: clean. Full `backend/tests` without a test database: 111 passed/1 skipped (unchanged — no new Python tests, only guard-test logic extended). Re-ran the same clean-venv simulation a second time after the `cache-dependency-path` fix (a fresh venv, same four commands in the same order): identical results (`make test-all`: 112 passed again) — the fix only changes what `setup-python` hashes for its cache key, not anything `make install`/`make lock-check`/`make lint`/`make test-all` actually do.
- 2026-09-25: Step 1.1.o.b — `uv pip compile --help` (installed uv 0.12.19) confirmed all four expected flag names exactly as anticipated: `--generate-hashes`, `--universal`, `--python-version`, `--extra`; no deviation to report. `backend/requirements.lock` generated with `uv pip compile backend/pyproject.toml --universal --python-version 3.12 --generate-hashes -o backend/requirements.lock` (23 packages, all hash-pinned, no sdist-only/unhashed fallback — no warnings in uv's output and every package entry verified by script to have ≥1 `--hash=sha256:` line). `backend/requirements-dev.lock` generated the same way plus `--extra dev` (59 packages, same verification; a superset of the runtime lock's package names, versions identical where shared — checked by script). `--universal` makes both files cross-platform (not tied to the resolving machine's OS/arch), matching the platform-specific packages (`greenlet`, `psycopg-binary`, `tzdata`) with their own environment markers rather than needing separate per-platform files. Every `==`-pinned dependency in `backend/pyproject.toml` (6 runtime, 6 dev) confirmed to match the corresponding lockfile's pinned version exactly, both by a one-off script and by the new `backend/tests/test_pyproject.py` tests. Verified: `pip install --require-hashes -r backend/requirements.lock` and `pip install --require-hashes -r backend/requirements-dev.lock` each succeed in their own clean Python 3.12.14 venv; in the dev venv, `pip install --no-deps -e ./backend` then `python -c "import app"` both succeed; `make lint` clean; `make test` (no test database) went from 106 passed/1 skipped to 110 passed/1 skipped (the 4 new lockfile-consistency tests), same 1 skip. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` has no reference to either lockfile yet (grep confirmed) — CI's `pip install -e "./backend[dev]"` step is unchanged, so this task does not alter CI behavior (that wiring is 1.1.o.e).

- 2026-09-26: Duplication check after 1.1.o.h/1.1b/1.1c: shared minimal_subprocess_env() test helper across test_hooks.py/test_evals_placeholder.py/test_alembic.py; fixed hooks.py to cleanly handle undecodable files (UnicodeDecodeError was not caught); A2/A3/B1/B2 declined as proportionate.

- 2026-09-26: Step 1.2 breakdown approved — six subtasks 1.2.a–1.2.f (see the Step 1.2 task list below), based on docs/SPEC.md §4.1 (tenants/site_keys/visitors/conversations columns), §4.5 (`thread_id = f"{client_id}:{vid}:{cid}"`), §5.4 (`plans.limits`), §13 (tenant deletion cascades), §14 (Postgres pools, Alembic as a separate deploy step) and §21 (tenant-scoped repository helpers), cross-checked against PROJECT_SPEC.md's own 1.2-owned open markers. Eight decisions confirmed: (1) models split per domain — `tenancy/models.py` (tenants, site_keys, visitors, conversations) and `plans/models.py` (plans) — matching the existing subpackage-per-domain convention (§21) rather than one shared models module; (2) UUID primary keys for `tenants.id`, `visitors.vid`, `conversations.cid` and `site_keys`' row id, since `client_id`/`vid`/`cid` are embedded directly in the JWT claim and in the `thread_id` string (§4.1, §4.5) and sequential integers would let one tenant enumerate another's rows; (3) `site_keys` gets its own synthetic UUID primary key, with `key` (the `pk_live_…` secret token) as a separate unique indexed column, not the PK — leaves room for future key rotation without changing the row's identity, though rotation itself is out of scope for v1; (4) DB-level `ON DELETE CASCADE` from `tenants` down to `site_keys`/`visitors`/`conversations` — the standard default (rule 11) and matches §13's eventual cascading-tenant-deletion requirement, cheaper to set correctly in the first migration than to retrofit; (5) `plans.limits` stays a minimal, unstructured JSONB column with no fixed sub-schema yet — most of §5.4's concrete limit fields belong to Phase 2/4 features not yet built, so giving them a schema now would be speculative (rule 11); (6) `users`/`memberships` are deferred entirely to Step 6.1 — §18's Phase 1 line names only tenants/site_keys/visitors/conversations/plans, and users/memberships first appear under 6.1 Dashboard authentication; (7) the DB readiness-check open marker stays owned solely by 1.4, not claimed by 1.2 — 1.2 builds schema and repository access only, and no DB-touching endpoint exists yet in 1.2 for a healthcheck to exercise; (8) pool sizing (§14's ~10 app/~5 worker figures) uses SQLAlchemy's engine defaults for now, with explicit pool-size configuration deferred to Phase 7 load testing, rather than hard-coding a number with no measured basis yet. Current task set to 1.2.a, next to 1.2.b.

- 2026-09-26: Step 1.2.a — async DB engine/session plumbing (`backend/app/db.py`). Driver-scheme finding, verified against the installed `sqlalchemy==2.0.54`/`psycopg==3.3.6` (not assumed): `DATABASE_URL`'s existing `postgresql+psycopg` scheme (psycopg 3, sync-driver form) needs **no change** for `create_async_engine()`. Confirmed by hand: psycopg 3 (unlike psycopg2) natively supports both sync and async connections through the one driver package; SQLAlchemy's psycopg dialect module exposes a `PGDialect_psycopg.get_async_dialect_cls()` classmethod for exactly this, and constructing `create_async_engine("postgresql+psycopg://...")` resolves to `PGDialectAsync_psycopg` (`engine.dialect.is_async is True`, `engine.dialect.driver == "psycopg"`) with no real connection attempted. `get_engine()`/`_session_factory()` are `@lru_cache`-wrapped (same lazy-singleton pattern as `get_settings()` itself) rather than built at raw module level: a literal top-level `create_async_engine(get_settings()...)` would call `get_settings()` at import time and violate the project's existing zero-import-side-effects convention (`get_settings()` raises `SettingsError` on an invalid/empty environment) — confirmed `create_async_engine()` itself is lazy (no connection until first checkout), but `get_settings()` is not, so the laziness has to be applied at the `get_engine()` level, not assumed to come free from SQLAlchemy alone. `get_db_session()` is a plain async-generator "dependency with yield" function (FastAPI's own documented pattern for DB sessions), not `@asynccontextmanager`-wrapped, so it plugs into `Depends()` unchanged later; non-FastAPI callers (e.g. 1.2.d's repository) drive it with `contextlib.aclosing()`. Pool size left at SQLAlchemy's defaults per the 1.2-breakdown decision (Phase 7 tunes it). One deviation from the task's stated file list, necessary and applied: `backend/tests/test_config_guard.py`'s `ALLOWED_DATABASE_URL_CALLERS` gained `"app/db.py"` (one line) — the pre-existing guard test would otherwise fail, since `db.py` is the second file (after `alembic/env.py`) allowed to call `database_url_str()`. Test design note: `db.py`'s `@lru_cache` engine is a correct process-lifetime singleton for the real app (one event loop for the process), but pytest-asyncio's default function-scoped event loop means each test gets a new loop, so `backend/tests/test_db.py`'s `_fresh_engine` fixture clears the cache and disposes the engine per test — test-harness plumbing only, confirmed via pytest-asyncio's own source (`asyncio_default_test_loop_scope` defaults to `"function"`, unchanged by this project's `pyproject.toml`). Verified: `make lint` clean; full `backend/tests` without a test database: **122 passed, 3 skipped** (up from 120 passed, 1 skipped — the new `test_db.py`'s three tests: two skip cleanly via `require_test_database()`, one — the no-side-effects import check — needs no database and ran for real); `make test-all` against the real test-db: **125 passed** (all three previously-skipped DB tests ran for real, including the 20-session leak check asserting `pool.checkedout() == 0`); `git status --short` showed exactly the expected three files. New open marker added (§9): nothing calls `get_engine().dispose()` at shutdown yet, since no app lifecycle hook exists until a real consumer wires one up. No ASSUMPTION/UNCERTAIN markers needed — the driver-scheme and laziness questions were resolved by direct verification, not left as assumptions.

- 2026-09-26: Step 1.2.b — ORM models (`backend/app/tenancy/models.py`: tenants, site_keys, visitors, conversations; `backend/app/plans/models.py`: plans). Shared declarative `Base` placed in `backend/app/db.py` (extending 1.2.a's module, per the task's own suggestion) rather than a new file — a two-line class not worth a third shared-infra module (rule 11). Two factual claims verified for real against the pinned `postgres:18.2-trixie` image (not assumed), both confirmed true: (1) `gen_random_uuid()` works with **zero extensions installed** (`pg_extension` showed only `plpgsql`) — moved into Postgres core in PG13+, so every UUID PK uses `server_default=text("gen_random_uuid()")`, generated at the DB layer regardless of which process writes a row; (2) Postgres does **not** auto-index foreign-key columns (unlike MySQL/InnoDB) — a live `CREATE TABLE ... REFERENCES` test showed only the referenced table's own PK index existed, nothing on the FK column — confirming every `tenant_id` (and `site_keys.key`) needed an explicit `index=True`, which all five tables now have. `status` columns use plain `String`, not a native Postgres `ENUM` type, for migration flexibility (adding an enum value needs `ALTER TYPE`, more operationally awkward than a plain string); `site_keys.status` gets an explicit `CheckConstraint` restricting it to `draft`/`live`/`suspended` since docs/SPEC.md §4.1 names that exact closed set, but `tenants.status` gets no constraint since no vocabulary is given anywhere in the spec (flagged as an ASSUMPTION in the model and as a new open marker, owner not yet scheduled). `tenants.plan_id` is nullable (§5.4: "plans are assigned manually," implying a tenant can exist before one is assigned). `plans.id` is a UUID (not a fixed string/enum id) for consistency with every other table's PK style, rather than a second PK scheme for one table (rule 11). Cascade scope clarification: the earlier 1.2-breakdown decision ("DB-level ON DELETE CASCADE from tenants down to site_keys/visitors/conversations") covers only FKs *to* `tenants` — built as `ondelete="CASCADE"` on all three `tenant_id` columns, confirmed against the real database (`pg_constraint.confdeltype = 'c'` on all three). `visitors.site_key_id` and `conversations.vid` are a separate edge that decision never named and no delete feature needs yet, so both were left at the default (`NO ACTION`, confirmed `confdeltype = 'a'`) — this diverges from this task's own verification wording ("presence of ON DELETE CASCADE on each tenant_id/site_key_id/vid foreign key"), which would have cascaded all three FK kinds; `backend/tests/test_models.py` asserts what was actually built (cascade only on `tenant_id`), not the verification text literally, and this discrepancy is called out here rather than silently resolved either way. `backend/alembic/env.py`: `target_metadata = Base.metadata`, importing both `models` modules for their table-registration side effect (`# noqa: F401`); the `TODO(1.2)` comment is removed (resolved) along with its open marker. Real-database proof (throwaway, discarded): with the test-db, `alembic revision --autogenerate` detected all 5 tables plus every explicit index cleanly, no errors; `alembic upgrade head` applied the generated DDL successfully (proving the JSONB/ARRAY server-side defaults are valid SQL, not just importable Python); `alembic downgrade base` cleanly reverted it; the revision file was deleted and never committed. Verified: `make lint` clean; full `backend/tests` without a test database: **129 passed, 3 skipped** (up from 122 passed, 3 skipped — the 7 new model-only tests in `test_models.py`, none needing a database); `git status --short` showed exactly the expected five files (`backend/alembic/env.py`, `backend/app/db.py` modified; `backend/app/tenancy/models.py`, `backend/app/plans/models.py`, `backend/tests/test_models.py` new).

- 2026-09-27: Step 1.2.d — tenant-scoped repository core (`backend/app/tenancy/repository.py`, `TenantScopedRepository`). Immutability mechanism: `@dataclass(frozen=True)`, the standard-library mechanism (rule 11 — a well-known mechanism over a hand-rolled `__setattr__` override); any post-`__init__` attribute assignment raises `dataclasses.FrozenInstanceError` (confirmed by hand to be a subclass of `AttributeError`), verified directly rather than assumed. Fields are named plainly (`tenant_id`, `session`, no leading underscore): the enforcement comes from `frozen=True` itself, not from hiding the name, and a plain name keeps the constructor call (`TenantScopedRepository(tenant_id=..., session=...)`) idiomatic. `tenant_id` has no default (omitting it raises Python's own `TypeError`); `__post_init__` additionally rejects an explicit `tenant_id=None` (`ValueError`), since a required field only guarantees the argument was passed, not that it's a real value. Two concrete methods prove the pattern: `get_conversation_by_id(cid)` and `list_site_keys()`, both filtering on `self.tenant_id` unconditionally — no method anywhere on the class accepts a `tenant_id` parameter. One necessary deviation from the task's stated file list, flagged before building: `backend/tests/test_app_skeleton.py`'s `STUB_ENTRY_POINTS` had to drop its `("app.tenancy.repository", "get_tenant_scoped_repository")` entry, since that stub function no longer exists / no longer raises `NotImplementedError` once real functionality replaced it — same kind of pre-existing-guard-test fix as 1.2.a's `ALLOWED_DATABASE_URL_CALLERS` edit. Real bug caught by running the new test file standalone rather than trusting the full-suite run: `backend/tests/test_tenancy_repository.py` initially passed only inside `make test-all` by accident, because `test_models.py`/`test_alembic.py` (imported earlier in the same pytest session) had already registered `app.plans.models`'s `plans` table onto the shared `Base.metadata` — the new file never imported `app.plans.models` itself, so `Base.metadata.create_all()` failed with `NoReferencedTableError` on `tenants.plan_id` when run in isolation. Fixed by adding the same side-effect import (`from app.plans import models as plans_models  # noqa: F401`) already used in `alembic/env.py` and `test_models.py`. Security proof, against the real test database (two tenants, one site_key and one conversation each): `list_site_keys()` scoped to tenant A returns only tenant A's site key; `get_conversation_by_id(tenant_B_cid)` — a real, valid row belonging to the wrong tenant — returns `None`, not tenant B's conversation; `get_conversation_by_id(tenant_A_cid)` correctly returns tenant A's own conversation, proving the mechanism isn't just blocking everything. Verified: `make lint` clean; full `backend/tests` without a test database: **133 passed, 8 skipped** (up from 130 passed, 5 skipped — 3 new structural tests run for real, 3 new security-proof tests skip as expected); `make test-all` against the real test-db: **141 passed** (133 + the 8 previously-skipped, all for real, re-run clean after the metadata-import fix); `git status --short` showed exactly the expected files (`backend/app/tenancy/repository.py` modified, `backend/tests/test_tenancy_repository.py` new, plus the flagged `test_app_skeleton.py` fix). `tenancy/repository.py`'s `TODO(1.2)` marker resolved and its open-markers line removed.

- 2026-09-27: Step 1.2.e — CRUD/lookup functions on `TenantScopedRepository`, plus `create_tenant()`. Tenant-creation location: `create_tenant(session, name, status) -> Tenant`, a plain module-level function in `backend/app/tenancy/repository.py`, deliberately never a method on `TenantScopedRepository` — a brand-new tenant has no `tenant_id` yet to scope by, which the class's own constructor invariant (always requires a real `tenant_id`) cannot represent, so a separate function makes "this is the one unscoped operation" structurally visible rather than a special-cased method fighting the class's own guarantee. site_key_id/vid verification: `create_visitor(site_key_id, secret_hash)` and `create_conversation(vid, title=None)` both verify the given foreign id belongs to `self.tenant_id` before creating the row (raising `ValueError` otherwise), rather than trusting the FK constraints already on those columns (`visitors.site_key_id -> site_keys.id`, `conversations.vid -> visitors.vid`) to be enough — a foreign key only proves the referenced row *exists*, not that it belongs to the same tenant, and nothing else in the codebase yet guarantees that (1.4's session endpoint, the only other place that would touch this, doesn't exist yet), so skipping the check would silently accept a tenant-A visitor pointing at tenant B's site_key, or a tenant-A conversation pointing at tenant B's visitor. `get_tenant()` returns the repository's own bound tenant via `scalar_one()` (not `_or_none()`), since a `TenantScopedRepository` is only ever constructed with a real tenant's id — a missing row there is a real error, not a normal "not found." `create_site_key()` always creates in `"draft"` status (docs/SPEC.md §4.1/§6.3: a key moves to `live` only through a separate server-side check, not this method's job). Security proof, against the real test database (same two-tenant fixture as 1.2.d, extended with a visitor per tenant): `get_tenant()` scoped to A returns only A; `create_site_key`/`create_visitor`/`create_conversation` all set `tenant_id` automatically from `self.tenant_id`; `create_visitor` given tenant B's real `site_key_id` and `create_conversation` given tenant B's real `vid` both raise `ValueError`, not silently succeed; `get_visitor_by_id` mirrors `get_conversation_by_id`'s hostile-caller/own-row pair; `create_tenant()` proved to create a real, independently-verifiable row with no tenant scoping involved, and confirmed absent from `TenantScopedRepository` via `hasattr`. Caught by running the extended test file standalone before trusting the full-suite run (same lesson as 1.2.d): none this time — the existing `plans` side-effect import was already in place from 1.2.d's fix, so the new tests passed standalone on the first try.
