### docs/SPEC.md §16 acceptance record (Phase 1)

This is PROJECT_SPEC.md's own acceptance record — not a restatement of
docs/SPEC.md §16 itself — naming which task proved each line, and which
future step owns each undone clause. Only the "Identity and threads" and
"Abuse and cost" groups apply to what exists through Step 1.6; every other
§16 group (Ingestion and crawler, Escalation, Streaming, Injection,
Dashboard) has no built code yet anywhere in this project and is not
applicable to Phase 1 at all.

**Identity and threads:**
- "A tenant A token can never return tenant B's chunks, including through the hybrid query's dense and sparse sub-queries." — **CONFIRMED ACCEPTED** for the underlying Qdrant filter/index isolation mechanism (1.3.d; re-verified standalone, real output, at 1.6.c: `test_qdrant_isolation.py`, 16 passed). **DEFERRED**: the token/`retrieve()`-mediated version → **Step 3.1** (no `retrieve()` exists yet).
- "Visitor A can't read or resume visitor B's conversation, even within the same tenant." — **DEFERRED** in full → **Step 4.2** (Conversations endpoints and retention sweep). `get_conversation_by_id()` (1.2) filters only by `tenant_id`, not `vid` — visitor-level enforcement has no endpoint to live in yet.
- "Another visitor's `conversation_id` returns 404, and client-sent `thread_id`/`client_id`/`configurable` is ignored." — **DEFERRED** in full → **Step 3.2** (checkpointer) / **Step 4.1** (`/chat`) — neither exists.
- "`/api/v1/session` from a non-allowlisted or missing `Origin` returns 403, and a token replayed with a different `Origin` returns 401." — **CONFIRMED ACCEPTED** (1.4.g, 1.4.h; `test_session.py`, `test_dependencies.py`; re-verified fresh at 1.6.c).
- "Expired, forged, `alg=none` and wrong-`aud` tokens return 401, and a widget token on admin endpoints returns 401." — **CONFIRMED ACCEPTED** for the first clause (1.4.h). **DEFERRED**: "a widget token on admin endpoints" → **Phase 6** (no admin endpoints exist).
- "A model-generated tool call containing `client_id` has no effect." — **DEFERRED** in full → **Step 3.6** (tool wrapper doesn't exist).
- "A suspended tenant is blocked within the cache TTL, and the retention sweep deletes expired chats and nothing newer." — **CONFIRMED ACCEPTED** for the first clause (1.4.h's `StatusCache` TTL test). **DEFERRED**: "the retention sweep" → **Step 4.2**.

**Abuse and cost:**
- "The 11th message in a minute returns 429 with `Retry-After`, and a second concurrent stream is refused." — **DEFERRED** in full → **Steps 4.1/4.3** (per 1.5.f's own marker; `/chat` doesn't exist).
- "A session-creation flood from one IP is blocked, and many new visitors from one IP hit the IP cap." — **CONFIRMED ACCEPTED** in full (1.5.c, 1.5.e; `test_session.py`; re-verified fresh at 1.6.c).
- Every remaining "Abuse and cost" line (100% quota, fallback/escalation exemption, restart reload, the 503-during-reload window, SMTP retry, trace-text-at-30-days) — **DEFERRED** in full → **Steps 4.3/4.4/4.5** (quota, escalation and tracing systems don't exist).

### Phase 1 closure (2026-09-30)

PROJECT_SPEC.md's own Components and status table (§4) has no phase-level
row or status field — only per-step rows. This section itself is the
record that Phase 1 is complete; no new status field is added to §4 for
it, to avoid a second, redundant place that could drift out of sync.

**What each component delivered** (one line each, a rollup — not a
re-derivation of any step's own closure summary above):
- **1.1 Repo skeleton and Compose**: a hardened, non-root Docker image; a Postgres+Qdrant+api+worker Compose stack; a two-job CI workflow; hash-pinned lockfiles; weekly + change-triggered dependency/image security scanning.
- **1.1b Whole-application skeleton**: 12 subpackage stub modules (one per docs/SPEC.md §21 subpackage), each raising `NotImplementedError` with a `TODO(<owning step>)`, proving the repo layout matches the spec's own module map before any real feature exists.
- **1.1c Marker check**: `make hooks`, a marker-visibility script (TODO/ASSUMPTION/UNCERTAIN/NOTE), wired into the Makefile.
- **1.2 Database, migrations and tenant-scoped access**: 5 ORM models; the first Alembic migration, hand-verified against a live database; `TenantScopedRepository`, the sole enforcement point for "all tenant-scoped database access goes through the tenant-scoped repository helpers" (§21), proven via cross-tenant hostile-caller isolation tests.
- **1.3 Qdrant collection [SECURITY]**: an authenticated Qdrant service; idempotent collection-schema setup (`ensure_collection()`); a strict `tenant_filter()`; two-tenant isolation proofs across dense, sparse and hybrid queries; an import-confinement read-path guard.
- **1.4 Session endpoint and JWT [SECURITY]**: `POST /api/v1/session` issuing rotatable HS256 JWTs; a verified-identity dependency with a TTL-cached tenant/site-key status check; the repository dependency completing the chain from a verified JWT to an automatically tenant-scoped query.
- **1.5 Rate limiter and limits**: a pure, framework-agnostic sliding-window-log `RateLimiter`; validated Settings-driven limit/window configuration; both a per-`(IP, site_key)` and a per-IP counter wired into `POST /api/v1/session`, with a real `Retry-After` countdown and a proven collision-safe key encoding.
- **1.6 Phase 1 acceptance**: see its own closure summary immediately above.

**docs/SPEC.md §18's own Phase 1 accept line**, quoted exactly:
> "`docker compose up` gives a healthy stack; session flow and identity tests pass."

**This line is now satisfied.** Evidence: the first clause by 1.6.a's live
Compose health check (a fresh stack, from `.env.example`'s own defaults,
reaches healthy with no hidden dependency on `ensure_collection()`); the
second clause by 1.6.c's fresh, independent full-suite acceptance gate
(a re-run of it, fresh again, at this close-out: **306 passed, 104
skipped** without a database; **410 passed** with `make test-all`, both
test services confirmed cleaned up) plus 1.6.d's new end-to-end test
proving a real session-issued token carries identity all the way through
to an actual tenant-scoped database read. The full line-by-line
docs/SPEC.md §16 acceptance status is recorded immediately above — not
repeated here.

### Step 2.1 closure summary (2026-10-04)

Built: `sources`/`documents`/`db_connections`/`jobs` (UUID PKs, tenant-cascading
FKs), with `db_connections`' credentials pgcrypto-encrypted at rest behind a
single guarded accessor (`get_decrypted_credentials()`), AST-guard-enforced
just like `qdrant_api_key_str()`; a `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` job
queue (enqueue/claim/mark) proven safe under 13 real concurrent claimers,
with exponential backoff and a separate immediate-failure path for
structurally unrecoverable errors (an unknown `job_type`); a real worker
processing loop (claim → dispatch → mark) with proven cooperative graceful
shutdown and a file-based heartbeat healthcheck distinguishing idle-alive
from wedged; and `ensure_collection()` (built at 1.3.c) finally wired into
the deploy path via the `migrate` service, live-proven to create the
`knowledge_chunks` collection on a fresh stack and verify-only on a rerun.

Two real security bugs were caught and fixed mid-step, not just busywork:
a database credential round-trip that only worked by accident of test
ordering (2.1.a, fixed by moving `pgcrypto`'s creation into the real
migration itself); and `worker.py`'s per-iteration failure handler logging
full exception messages via `exc_info=True` despite its own comment
claiming otherwise — a latent credential-leak risk, closed and proven with
a live log-capture test at the 2.1.c/d/e duplication check.

See Open markers below for what remains outstanding, not repeated here.

### Step 2.2 task list (approved)

Nine subtasks, 2.2.a–2.2.i (one more letter than originally proposed: decision (d) below splits the single "revocation" subtask into two — the temporary admin-key mechanism, and `revoke_domain()` itself — pushing close-out from 2.2.h to 2.2.i).

| ID | Goal | Status |
|----|------|--------|
| 2.2.a | `verified_domains` ORM model (`tenant_id`-indexed, `ON DELETE CASCADE` from `tenants`, matching every existing table's own treatment): `status` a `CheckConstraint` of exactly `pending`/`verified`/`revoked`; `method` a `CheckConstraint` of exactly `dns` (file/meta_tag added back once Step 2.3 lands, per the Open marker below); a per-attempt `verification_token`; `verified_at`/`revoked_at` nullable. `audit_log` ORM model: `actor` a plain string column (no FK — no real admin-identity system exists until Step 6.1), `action`/`target_type`/`target_id`/`details` (JSONB)/`created_at`, append-only by design (enforced at 2.2.b, not here) — new `backend/app/ingest/models.py` additions | Done |
| 2.2.b | Alembic migration for both tables, autogenerate reviewed by hand (matching 2.1.b's own discipline). `audit_log`'s append-only invariant enforced here, at the database level, as the **primary** mechanism: `REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE ON audit_log FROM <app role>` (exact statement/role confirmed live at build time, matching this project's own "verify at build time, don't assume" precedent) | Done |
| 2.2.c | Tenant-scoped claim primitives (`start_verification(tenant_id, domain, method) -> token`, read/list own domains), mirroring `TenantScopedRepository`'s own pattern. Enforces **global uniqueness**: a domain already claimed by another tenant in `pending` or `verified` status rejects a second tenant's claim attempt outright; a `revoked` domain is claimable again by any tenant, including a different one. Hostile-caller tests: tenant A can never read/write/claim over tenant B's row (mirroring 1.2.d/2.1.a exactly); a second tenant's claim on an already-pending-or-verified domain is rejected; the same domain becomes claimable by a different tenant once revoked | Done |
| 2.2.d | DNS verification mechanics: introduces `dnspython` as a new runtime dependency (rule 8; exact version re-verified live on PyPI at build time, not assumed from this planning session, matching every prior dependency addition's own precedent) — a TXT-record lookup and a pure, offline-testable comparison against the claim's own per-attempt token | Done |
| 2.2.e | `confirm_verification(domain_id)`: drives 2.2.d's DNS check end-to-end for a `pending` claim, sets `status="verified"`/`verified_at` on success, idempotent re-attempt behavior (matching §5.5's "verified once, not re-checked" — a successful call is a no-op on an already-`verified` row, not a second check). Primitive only, no endpoint (no dashboard exists yet to call one — matching 2.1's own "tables and primitives, not the full feature" precedent) | Done |
| 2.2.f | Temporary admin-key mechanism: a new required `Settings.admin_api_key` (`SecretStr`, same validation discipline as `qdrant_api_key`/`jwt_signing_key`) plus a guarded `admin_api_key_str()` accessor and `test_config_guard.py` allow-list entry; a verified-admin dependency (`require_admin_key()`, `app/admin/dependencies.py`, reading a dedicated `X-Admin-Key` header — not `Authorization`, deliberately, so a widget JWT is never even looked at — compared via `hmac.compare_digest`, matching `visitors.secret_hash`'s own constant-time-comparison precedent), returning an empty `AdminKeyVerified` marker on success. Explicitly scoped as a minimal, temporary mechanism — superseded outright by Step 6.1's real `platform_admin` role, not extended or built on later. Field/header names deviate from this breakdown's own original wording (`admin_key`/"`Authorization`-header or similar") — see this task's own decision-log entry for why | Done |
| 2.2.g | `revoke_domain(session, domain_id, actor)`: sets `status="revoked"`/`revoked_at`, writes an `audit_log` row in the same flush, takes effect immediately (no cache, no delay). **A primitive only, deliberately** — module-level function (`app/ingest/repository.py`), not wired to any endpoint. The endpoint wiring (`app/admin/routes.py`, protected by 2.2.f's dependency, the hostile-caller test against it) this row originally named is **confirmed dropped from Step 2.2 entirely** — built, if ever, under Step 6.1's real admin-identity system instead, never under the temporary `admin_api_key` mechanism; see the Step 6.1 Open marker and this task's own decision-log entry | Done |
| 2.2.h | `audit_log` write-path guard: an AST-based allow-list (matching `test_config_guard.py`/`test_qdrant_read_path_guard.py`'s own style) restricting which file may ever construct an `audit_log` row. **Corrected from this row's own original wording** (written before 2.2.b's own superuser-REVOKE-is-inert finding): this guard is **today's ONLY real enforcement** against `audit_log` mutation, not a secondary layer under a working DB-level `REVOKE` — see this task's own decision-log entry | Done |
| 2.2.i | Close-out: resolve the Step-2.2-owned markers (the `verified_domains`/`audit_log` deferral from 2.1.a — never a standalone Open marker, inherently satisfied by 2.2.a onward actually building those tables, confirmed at close-out; the file/meta-tag marker this breakdown adds below — correctly left open, owned by Step 2.3, not resolvable yet), update PROJECT_SPEC.md | Done |

### Step 2.2 closure summary (2026-10-04)

Built: `verified_domains`/`audit_log`, a partial unique index enforcing
global cross-tenant domain uniqueness while allowing reclaim after
revocation; DNS-only ownership verification (`dnspython`,
pure-function-plus-thin-IO-wrapper); `confirm_verification()`'s state
machine, never re-checking an already-verified domain and never silently
verifying on a transient DNS failure; `revoke_domain()`, a deliberately
unscoped admin primitive proving cross-tenant write access is *correct*
here, not a regression; a temporary, structurally-distinct admin-key
mechanism marked for outright replacement at Step 6.1; the `audit_log`
write-path guard — this step's final piece and, until Phase 7's role
split, the ONLY real enforcement against audit-row tampering.

Four real findings, not busywork: a second `create_site_key()`
rollback gap; the superuser-REVOKE discovery, which reversed Step 2.2's
own enforcement-ordering decision and is why the AST guard matters today;
a genuine docs/SPEC.md-vs-PROJECT_SPEC.md conflict, resolved then the
resulting endpoint dropped as premature; and a 2.2.h/2.2.i numbering
mismatch, caught by this very close-out process before Step 2.2 could
close with its own stated sole enforcement unbuilt.

See Open markers below for what remains outstanding, not repeated here.

### Step 2.3 task list (approved)

Five subtasks, 2.3.a–2.3.e, in build order. A reusable, generic primitive
only — no real caller wired here, matching `confirm_verification()`/
`revoke_domain()`'s own precedent; the first real caller is file/meta-tag
domain verification (2.3.e resolves that Open marker), not 2.6.

| ID | Goal | Status |
|----|------|--------|
| 2.3.a | IP-classification primitive (pure, offline, no network): a fail-closed function over both IPv4 and IPv6 (`not ip.is_global or ip.is_multicast or ip.is_reserved or ip.is_unspecified`) rejecting RFC 1918, loopback, link-local, the cloud-metadata address, IPv6 unique-local/loopback/link-local, CGNAT (`100.64.0.0/10`) and multicast. Table-driven offline tests, the metadata IP as its own named case. **Built in `app/ingest/ip_safety.py`, not `safe_fetch.py`** — see this task's own decision-log entry for why | Done |
| 2.3.b | DNS-resolve-and-validate: resolves a hostname to every returned IP (both families), validates ALL of them via 2.3.a, rejects the whole hostname if any is unsafe (fail closed — never connects via an unvalidated IP from the same answer). Hostile-proof tests: NXDOMAIN (matching 2.2.d's own convention); a monkeypatched resolver simulating DNS rebinding (an otherwise-innocuous hostname resolving to a private/metadata IP). **Built in a new `app/ingest/host_safety.py`, not `ip_safety.py`** — see this task's own decision-log entry for why | Done |
| 2.3.c | The single-fetch primitive (no redirect following yet): scheme check (http/https only); `httpx` promoted from dev-only to a direct runtime dependency here (`httpx==0.28.1`, re-verified current on PyPI at the research stage, not assumed); connects to the validated IP literal (never the hostname), `Host` header and `extensions={"sni_hostname": ...}` set to the original hostname for correct TLS SNI/cert verification; size cap enforced via streaming (never buffers an oversized body first); configurable timeout. Defaults match docs/SPEC.md §5.5 exactly: 5MB, 15s (**the redirect-count cap is 2.3.d's own parameter, not this function's — corrected from this row's own original wording, which listed it here; `follow_redirects=False` means this function never needs one**). Hostile-proof test: a literal private/loopback/metadata URL rejected before any connection attempt, with proof of zero bytes sent | Done |
| 2.3.d | Redirect-chain handling: wraps 2.3.c in a loop — each 3xx response re-runs the ENTIRE 2.3.a→2.3.b→2.3.c validation cycle on the new `Location` target from scratch, never carrying a prior hop's validation forward. Hard cap at the configured redirect count (default 3), failing closed past it. Hostile-proof test: a chain starting at a validated-safe address that redirects to an unsafe one at hop 2 (not hop 1), caught at that exact hop; an excessive-redirect-count chain rejected | Done |
| 2.3.e | Close-out: restore `file`/`meta_tag` to `verified_domains.method`'s `CheckConstraint` (new migration), resolving the one Step-2.2-owned Open marker naming this step; confirm docs/SPEC.md §16's acceptance line ("blocked, including through redirects and DNS rebinding") is covered end-to-end by 2.3.b–d's own live tests; update PROJECT_SPEC.md | Done |

### Step 2.3 closure summary (2026-10-05)

Built: a fail-closed, protocol-agnostic IP classifier reusable by Step
2.8; DNS resolution rejecting the whole hostname if any A/AAAA result is
unsafe, with DNS rebinding closed completely (connect-by-IP-literal
means no second DNS lookup, confirmed empirically); an IP-pinned fetch
with genuine TLS SNI/cert verification, proven live via a deliberately
wrong SNI causing a real handshake failure; streaming size enforcement
proven to abort mid-download by timing; full redirect-chain
re-validation per hop, with a live zero-connection proof for the unsafe
case; and, here, the schema half of file/meta-tag verification restored,
live-proven through upgrade→downgrade→upgrade.

Two real findings: the literal-IP-to-DNS-query gap caught building 2.3.c
(would have leaked a real query for a zero-network-activity target); and
the `follow_redirects` enforcement gap caught by the 2.3.a/b/c
duplication check, proven live and locked in before 2.3.d's own design
came to depend on it.

See Open markers below (file/meta-tag verification LOGIC still has no
owner) — not repeated here.

Tested against 2.4.b's own real extracted output, not only synthetic text. New dependency: `tiktoken` | Done |

### Step 2.4 closure summary (2026-10-05)

Built: `documents` schema completion (partial uniqueness, a `status`
vocabulary check); a fail-closed HTML extractor with script/style/
hidden-content stripping proven never to leak; a deterministic, token-
proven 512/64 chunker on `tiktoken`, with `content_hash` over
normalized text for idempotent re-runs; PDF/DOCX/TXT/MD extractors, each
shaped to its own format's real structure, all five sharing one
unforced `ExtractedContent`; a durable lockfile-tooling fix (`uv`'s
`-c`/`--constraints`) preventing a whole class of future cross-lockfile
conflicts, not just the one that triggered it.

Real findings: the document-order `heading_path` stack algorithm,
worked out for HTML, proven to transfer cleanly to DOCX and Markdown
with no rework; `cl100k_base`'s own runtime network-fetch behavior (not
bundled in `tiktoken`), flagged for Phase 7; the `uv -c` mechanism
itself, a structural fix rather than a per-package pin.

2.6, "already-indexed content" now points to Step 2.5) — not repeated

Live-proven both directly (`backend/tests/test_queue.py`'s own `test_reap_stuck_jobs_*`, a job manually left `"running"` with a stale `updated_at`, reclaimed correctly; a genuinely fresh `"running"` job left alone; the max-attempts ceiling reached correctly through the reaper) and through the real loop (`backend/tests/test_worker.py`'s own `test_run_reaps_a_stuck_job_during_its_own_loop`/`test_run_leaves_a_genuinely_running_job_alone_during_its_own_loop`).

Raises `UnknownJobTypeError`, a plain pre-check exception, never reaching `worker.py`'s own unknown-`job_type` permanent-failure path for a job created through this sanctioned method. **`VALID_JOB_TYPES` is deliberately NOT the same thing as `JOB_HANDLERS`'s own key set, confirmed live, not assumed**: `JOB_HANDLERS` today is `{"noop", "sleep"}` — worker-loop-mechanics test scaffolding with no real caller, never meant to be part of a real, tenant/scheduler-facing vocabulary. The relationship going forward: `JOB_HANDLERS` must register a handler for every value in `VALID_JOB_TYPES` (so a real job never hits the permanent-failure path), but may always carry additional test-only entries this vocabulary never lists. A real, necessary consequence found and fixed in the same task: `test_worker.py` had 6 call sites relying on `enqueue()` accepting `"noop"`/`"sleep"`/deliberately-unregistered strings (`"not-a-registered-job-type"` etc., testing the WORKER's own unknown-type path specifically) — all 6 updated to construct the `Job` row directly, bypassing `enqueue()`, since all of them test worker dispatch behavior given a pre-existing row, not `enqueue()`'s own validation; confirmed via a full standalone run showing identical pass/fail behavior on every test, touched or not.

This must switch to a trusted forwarded header (and the proxy configured to set it reliably, stripping any client-supplied value) once that proxy is actually built. Not addressed now since building header-trust logic against infrastructure that doesn't exist yet would be speculative (rule 11).

- Owner: whoever first has reason to doubt it, or Phase 7's own full security pass (recorded at Task 2.7.b, 2026-10-07): `sniff_file_type()`'s own zip-bomb defense (`app/ingest/upload_sniff.py`) checks `zipfile.ZipInfo.file_size`, a DECLARED metadata field, which a sufficiently adversarial ZIP could in principle make inconsistent with its real compressed stream (lying about the size while keeping a self-consistent CRC for a larger real payload) — investigated live (hand-tampering a real ZIP's own size field), confirmed stdlib `zipfile` still catches many such inconsistencies via its own CRC-32 check during `read()`, but only AFTER decompressing, which would not prevent the resource cost. A deliberate v1 scope limit (rule 11 — the standard, well-known mitigation for the actual classic-zip-bomb risk being defended against, not the more exotic metadata-lying variant; no evidence yet that a real upload needs the latter closed), not a silent gap. Closing it fully would need a bounded, incremental decompressing read ignoring declared metadata entirely, mirroring pypdf's own `max_length=`-bounded approach.
- Owner: whoever first observes a real upload job hang near the 30-minute boundary, or Phase 7 (recorded at Task 2.7.b, 2026-10-07): no per-extraction-call wall-clock timeout was built around `extract_pdf()`/`extract_docx()`/`extract_text()`/`extract_markdown()`, despite the task explicitly raising the question. Reasoned through, not overlooked: the two real pathology categories found (PDF's circular-page-tree/decompression-bomb risks; DOCX's zip-bomb risk) are now closed by mechanisms that prevent the dangerous operation from STARTING at all, which a timeout cannot improve on; a Python synchronous-code timeout cannot actually GUARANTEE termination of a blocking C-level call or a live thread, so it would not genuinely bound memory (the real risk) even if built; and `reap_stuck_jobs()` (2.6.f, `job_stuck_after_seconds`, default 1800s) already provides a real, job-type-agnostic backstop against any job hanging abnormally long, for any reason. Revisit if a real upload job is ever observed to approach that 30-minute window in practice, which would be real evidence a per-extraction bound is worth the engineering cost a correct one (process-level, not signal/thread-based) would actually require.
- Owner: whoever next touches `app/ingest/extract_docx.py` (recorded at Task 2.7.c, 2026-10-08): that file's own header comment (2.4.e) claims `docx.opc.exceptions.PackageNotFoundError` covers "certain other package-level failures" beyond `zipfile.BadZipFile` — confirmed INCOMPLETE by building two real, reachable counter-examples live: a docx-shaped ZIP missing required OPC parts (`[Content_Types].xml`/`_rels/.rels`) raises a bare `KeyError`, not `PackageNotFoundError`; a complete, otherwise-valid `.docx` with its own `word/document.xml` content replaced by invalid XML raises `lxml.etree.XMLSyntaxError`, a third, undocumented type. Not fixed in `extract_docx.py` itself at 2.7.c (that task's own file list only pre-approved its header comment's mechanical "Step 2.6"→"Step 2.7" correction, per rule 2) — `app/ingest/upload_adapter.py`'s own dispatch table was widened to catch both anyway, narrowly scoped to that one call site, so no real-world behavior is currently broken by this stale comment; only the comment's own claim needs correcting, whenever this file is next touched for any reason.
- Owner Step 2.9 (Scheduler, plans and reconcile), recorded at Task 2.7.c, 2026-10-08, mirroring the already-acknowledged "stale Qdrant points on content shrink" marker (2.5.d) for the identical reason: a tenant re-uploading changed content under the same `source_id`/filename gets a NEW `upload_id` (a new on-disk file, per `upload_storage.save()`'s own design, 2.7.a) — the PREVIOUS `upload_id`'s own on-disk bytes are never deleted anywhere, becoming permanently orphaned storage. `ingest_upload()` itself has no way to know a previous `upload_id` existed (that mapping is expected to live in `sources.config`, read only by 2.7.d's own job handler, not this per-call primitive) — fixing this needs whoever manages a source's own config transition to know there WAS a previous value and delete it, not a per-call ingestion primitive.
- Owner: whoever builds source-deletion side effects for uploads (no task scheduled yet — possibly Step 2.9, possibly 6.3 Sources screens; recorded at Task 2.7.c, 2026-10-08): the EXISTING source-deletion/disable mechanism (`revoke_domain()` and friends, 2.2.g) only ever touches `verified_domains` rows and Qdrant points (`source_id`-scoped deletion/hiding, docs/SPEC.md §5.3) — nothing anywhere deletes an upload source's own on-disk stored bytes (`upload_storage.py`) when its source is disabled or deleted. A real, previously-unidentified gap, found while designing `ingest_upload()`'s own data lifecycle, not yet a problem at today's scale (nothing currently runs source deletion for real against upload sources) but a genuine one before go-live.
- Owner: whoever first hits a real tenant site large enough to need it (no task scheduled yet, recorded at Task 2.6.e, 2026-10-07): `app/ingest/crawl.py`'s `discover_sitemap_urls()`/`parse_sitemap_xml()` only handle a flat `<urlset>` of `<url><loc>` entries — a `<sitemapindex>` of nested sitemap files (a real-world pattern large sites use to split one sitemap across many files) is NOT recursed into; such a site's own index file would currently be parsed for `<loc>` entries that point at OTHER sitemap files, not real content pages, and those would be treated as if they were ordinary page URLs. A deliberate v1 scope limit (rule 11 — no evidence yet that a real tenant site needs this), not a silent gap.
